Hi, Doctor. Thank you so much for fitting me in. I really appreciate it. Um, I hope I'm in the right place for this, and I don't want to waste your time, but... I guess I came in because I just can’t seem to shut my brain off anymore. 

I know everyone gets stressed, especially in IT management, but it's gotten to the point where... well, it starts with just one small concern. Like, maybe a client isn't responding to an email right away. That's normal, right? But then, within minutes, I’m absolutely convinced I’m going to lose the contract. And then... it's a bit embarrassing to admit, but my mind just races, and suddenly I'm panicking that I'll fall behind on my divorce payments, and that I'll just never get back to a real leadership role again. 

It just spirals out of nowhere. I'm sorry, I know that probably sounds like a bit of an overreaction. I hope I'm explaining this okay... is that the kind of thing you usually help people with?

Um, interfere? I mean, I really try my best to keep everything running smoothly. I don't want to be someone who just drops the ball, you know? I still show up and put the hours in.

And if a recruiter delays an email, even just by a day, it can feel like absolute proof that my whole career is just closing down. \n\nI try so hard to just be logical about it and snap out of it, but I can't seem to reel it back in once it starts. Am I making sense? I just hope I'm not blowing things completely out of proportion.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you feel anxious or worried most days, even when there isn’t anything specific happening to cause it?\n\nPatient: Um, most days? I mean, I guess I do feel a little on edge pretty often. But I really try not to let it get to me, you know? I don't want to be one of those people who just complains about everything when there are people out there with real problems. \n\nI suppose, if I'm being completely honest... yeah, it's there a lot of the time. Even on a quiet Sunday when I should just be relaxing, I'll catch myself feeling this sort of low-level dread. But I'm sure everyone feels that way sometimes, right? Especially with everything going on in the world. I just... I don't want to make it sound worse than it is. I hope that answers your question okay? I just want to make sure I'm giving you an accurate picture without, you know, exaggerating.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying about a variety of different things—like work, relationships, health, or everyday responsibilities—even when one specific problem gets resolved?\n\nPatient: Um, a variety of things? I guess, maybe a little bit. I mean, I don't think I worry about everything under the sun, you know? I try to keep things in perspective. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around obsessing over every little detail of my life. \n\nBut... well, I suppose even if I get a contract sorted out, I don't really feel relieved for very long. It's like my brain just looks for the next thing to focus on. Like, okay, work is fine, but what about the house? Or, you know, my health, or if I'm keeping up with friends enough. It just kind of shifts gears. I'm sure that's just a normal part of getting older and having responsibilities, right? Everyone has a lot on their plate. I just... I hope I'm not making it sound like I can't handle my life. Is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Can you describe how much control you feel you have over your anxiety and worries when they come up?\n\nPatient: Um, control? I mean, I try really hard to keep a lid on it, I promise I do. I don't want to sound like I'm completely falling apart or anything, because I know I need to just push through. But... if I'm being totally honest, that's actually kind of why I came in. \n\nI just... I can't seem to shut my brain off anymore. It's like I have zero brakes once it gets going. I really hope you don't think I'm just being weak or over-dramatic. I just... I try to stop it, I really do, but it just takes over. Does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: Has this anxiety or worry started to interfere with your ability to focus at work, enjoy your free time, or manage your daily responsibilities?\n\nPatient: Um, interfere? I mean, I really try my best to keep everything running smoothly. I don't want to be someone who just drops the ball, you know? I still show up and put the hours in. \n\nBut... if I have to be completely honest, and I know I'm supposed to be... it has been affecting things a bit. It's not that I'm slacking off, I promise! It's just... I get so stuck anticipating every single thing that could possibly go wrong, and I just kind of freeze up trying to prevent it all. \n\nAnd with my free time... well, I've sort of pulled back from playing soccer and hanging out with my friends. I'm just really embarrassed by how tense and irritable I've become. I don't want to snap at anyone or bring the mood down, so it's just easier to stay away. I hope that makes sense? Is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced sudden episodes where you feel intense fear or discomfort that peaks within minutes, maybe with symptoms like a pounding heart, feeling short of breath, or like you're losing control?\n\nPatient: Um, sudden episodes of intense fear? ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, how long? Well, I mean, I've always been a bit of a planner, you know? Someone who likes to be prepared. I think that's usually a good trait for a manager. But... this level of worry, where it feels like it's running me instead of me running it? 

I guess... if I'm looking back, it really started getting bad about thirteen months ago. That's... well, I hope you don't judge me for this, but that's when I was let go from my old job. I mean, it was a restructuring thing, not because I was terrible at what I did, I promise! But yeah, getting fired really threw me. 

I thought it would get better once I landed on my feet, and I did find some contract work, which is great, right? I'm really grateful for it. But... the worrying just never really let up. It's like my brain got stuck in that panicked mode from thirteen months ago and just forgot how to turn off. I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is, I really am trying to handle it. Does that timeline make sense for what you're looking for?

I'm just really embarrassed by how tense and irritable I've become. I don't want to snap at anyone or bring the mood down, so it's just easier to stay away. I hope that makes sense? I just want to be sure I'm giving you the right information, I really don't want to sound like a complete mess who can't do his job. Is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced sudden episodes where you feel intense fear or discomfort that peaks within minutes, maybe with symptoms like a pounding heart, feeling short of breath, or like you're losing control?\n\nPatient: Um, sudden episodes of intense fear? Like a panic attack or something? No, no, I don't think I've ever had anything like that. I mean, my heart might beat a little fast if I'm really stressed about a deadline, but that's just normal stress, right? I definitely haven't had anything where I felt like I couldn't breathe or was losing control. \n\nI really try to keep myself together, you know? Even when my brain is racing, I don't just completely lose it. I hope that's a good thing? I just want to make sure I'm answering you right. But no, I haven't had any of those sudden, intense episodes you're describing. I'm just dealing with the constant worrying, mostly. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, with the constant worrying and difficulty controlling your anxiety?\n\nPatient: Um, how long? But... this level of worry, where it feels like it's running me instead of me running it? \n\nI guess... if I'm looking back, it really started getting bad about thirteen months ago. That's... well, I hope you don't judge me for this, but that's when I was let go from my old job. I mean, it was a restructuring thing, not because I was terrible at what I did, I promise! But yeah, getting fired really threw me. \n\nI thought it would get better once I landed on my feet, and I did find some contract work, which is great, right? I'm really grateful for it. But... the worrying just never really let up. It's like my brain got stuck in that panicked mode from thirteen months ago and just forgot how to turn off. I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is, I really am trying to handle it. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, restless? I mean... I guess I do have a lot of energy, which can be a good thing, right? It keeps me moving. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around doing nothing. 

But... yeah, if I'm being completely honest, I do feel pretty wound up most of the day. It's like my shoulders are just permanently locked up, almost like I'm bracing myself for some kind of bad news, even when everything is perfectly fine. I hope that doesn't sound too crazy. 

And I've noticed... well, I tend to pace around the house a lot. Or, if I'm on a work call, my leg is just constantly bouncing. Even when there's absolutely no reason to be stressed, I just can't seem to sit still. It's not that I'm trying to be disruptive or anything, I promise! It's just this nervous energy I can't shake. Is that... is that the kind of restlessness you mean? I hope I'm explaining it right.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, sleep... well, I mean, I try to get a full eight hours, I really do. I know how important sleep is for staying sharp, especially with contract work. I don't want you to think I'm just staying up all night being irresponsible or anything. 

But... yeah, it can be a little tricky sometimes. I hope it's normal to have a little trouble winding down? Most nights, I just kind of lie there, and my brain starts replaying, like, answers I gave in interviews, or I'll just find myself staring at the clock at two or three in the morning. It's not that I'm trying to worry, it just sort of happens. 

And then, even when I do manage to get what should be a good amount of sleep... I wake up feeling exhausted. It's almost like I spent the whole night on call for a major system outage, you know? Just totally drained. I really hope I'm not making it sound like I'm complaining too much. Is that the kind of sleep trouble you're asking about? It just kind of shifts gears. I'm sure that's just a normal part of getting older and having responsibilities, right? Everyone has a lot on their plate. I just... I hope I'm not making it sound like I can't handle my life. Is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Can you describe how much control you feel you have over your anxiety and worries when they come up?\n\nPatient: Um, control? I mean, I try really hard to keep a lid on it, I promise I do. I don't want to sound like I'm completely falling apart or anything, because I know I need to just push through. But... if I'm being totally honest, that's actually kind of why I came in. \n\nI just... I can't seem to shut my brain off anymore. It's like I have zero brakes once it gets going. It'll start with just one little concern, you know? Like, a client not responding to an email. And I know I should just wait it out, but within minutes, my brain just runs away with it. I'll be completely convinced I'm going to lose the contract, and then I start panicking about falling behind on my divorce payments, and then I'm spiraling into this thought that I'll never get back to a real leadership role again. \n\nI know it sounds so irrational when I say it out loud. I really hope you don't think I'm just being weak or over-dramatic. I just... I try to stop it, I really do, but it just takes over. Does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: Has this anxiety or worry started to interfere with your ability to focus at work, enjoy your free time, or manage your daily responsibilities?\n\nPatient: Um, interfere? I mean, I really try my best to keep everything running smoothly. I don't want to be someone who just drops the ball, you know? I still show up and put the hours in. \n\nBut... if I have to be completely honest, and I know I'm supposed to be... it has been affecting things a bit. I hope you don't judge me for this, because I usually pride myself on my work, but I've actually missed a few networking follow-ups lately. And I've even delayed some client deliverables. It's not that I'm slacking off, I promise! It's just... I get so stuck anticipating every single thing that could possibly go wrong, and I just kind of freeze up trying to prevent it all. \n\nAnd with my free time... well, I've sort of pulled back from playing soccer and hanging out with my friends. I'm just really embarrassed by how tense and irritable I've become. I don't want to snap at anyone or bring the mood down, so it's just easier to stay away. I hope that makes sense? I just want to be sure I'm giving you the right information, I really don't want to sound like a complete mess who can't do his job. Is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced sudden episodes where you feel intense fear or discomfort that peaks within minutes, maybe with symptoms like a pounding heart, feeling short of breath, or like you're losing control?\n\nPatient: Um, sudden episodes of intense fear? Like a panic attack or something? No, no, I don't think I've ever had anything like that. I mean, my heart might beat a little fast if I'm really stressed about a deadline, but that's just normal stress, right? Even when my brain is racing, I don't just completely lose it. I hope that's a good thing? I just want to make sure I'm answering you right. Does that mean I'm doing okay in that area?\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you find that your anxiety has been affecting your ability to interact with others, like avoiding social situations or feeling like you can’t connect with people the way you used to?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 47 / computer_or_information_systems_manager / bachelors\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Functional Impairment\nmanifestation: \"I’ve missed networking follow-ups and delayed client deliverables because I get stuck anticipating everything that could go wrong, and I’ve pulled back from soccer and friends because I’m embarrassed by how tense and irritable I’ve become.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, social situations? I mean, I still try to be friendly and professional, I really do. I don't want you to think I'm just hiding in my house or being rude to people. I always try to keep up a good front.

But... yeah, I guess things have been a little harder lately. I hope it's okay to admit that? I've actually missed a few networking follow-ups recently... and, um, I've even delayed some client deliverables. It's not because I'm lazy or don't care about my work, I promise! It's just... I get so stuck anticipating every single little thing that could possibly go wrong, and I just freeze up. I really hope you don't think less of me for that.

And outside of work... well, I used to play soccer and hang out with friends a lot more. But lately, I've been pulling back from all of that. I just... I get so embarrassed by how tense and irritable I've been feeling, and I don't want to bring everyone else down, you know? I want to be the guy everyone can rely on, not the guy who's always stressed out. Is it normal to pull away like that when you're feeling this wound up? I hope I'm not doing too much damage to my relationships.

Even on a quiet Sunday when I should just be relaxing, I'll catch myself feeling this sort of low-level dread. But I'm sure everyone feels that way sometimes, right? I guess, maybe a little bit. I mean, I don't think I worry about everything under the sun, you know? I try to keep things in perspective. I hope I'm not making it sound like I can't handle my life. I'll be completely convinced I'm going to lose the contract, and then I start panicking about falling behind on my divorce payments, and then I'm spiraling into this thought that I'll never get back to a real leadership role again. \n\nI know it sounds so irrational when I say it out loud. I really hope you don't think I'm just being weak or over-dramatic. I just... I try to stop it, I really do, but it just takes over. Does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: Has this anxiety or worry started to interfere with your ability to focus at work, enjoy your free time, or manage your daily responsibilities?\n\nPatient: Um, interfere? I mean, I really try my best to keep everything running smoothly. I don't want to be someone who just drops the ball, you know? I still show up and put the hours in. \n\nBut... if I have to be completely honest, and I know I'm supposed to be... it has been affecting things a bit. I hope you don't judge me for this, because I usually pride myself on my work, but I've actually missed a few networking follow-ups lately. And I've even delayed some client deliverables. It's not that I'm slacking off, I promise! It's just... I get so stuck anticipating every single thing that could possibly go wrong, and I just kind of freeze up trying to prevent it all. \n\nAnd with my free time... well, I've sort of pulled back from playing soccer and hanging out with my friends. I'm just really embarrassed by how tense and irritable I've become. I don't want to snap at anyone or bring the mood down, so it's just easier to stay away. I hope that makes sense? I just want to be sure I'm giving you the right information, I really don't want to sound like a complete mess who can't do his job. Is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced sudden episodes where you feel intense fear or discomfort that peaks within minutes, maybe with symptoms like a pounding heart, feeling short of breath, or like you're losing control?\n\nPatient: Um, sudden episodes of intense fear? I mean, my heart might beat a little fast if I'm really stressed about a deadline, but that's just normal stress, right? I definitely haven't had anything where I felt like I couldn't breathe or was losing control. \n\nI really try to keep myself together, you know? Even when my brain is racing, I don't just completely lose it. I hope that's a good thing? I just want to make sure I'm answering you right. But no, I haven't had any of those sudden, intense episodes you're describing. I'm just dealing with the constant worrying, mostly. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, with the constant worrying and difficulty controlling your anxiety?\n\nPatient: Um, how long? Well, I mean, I've always been a bit of a planner, you know? Someone who likes to be prepared. I think that's usually a good trait for a manager. But... this level of worry, where it feels like it's running me instead of me running it? \n\nI guess... if I'm looking back, it really started getting bad about thirteen months ago. That's... well, I hope you don't judge me for this, but that's when I was let go from my old job. I mean, it was a restructuring thing, not because I was terrible at what I did, I promise! But yeah, getting fired really threw me. \n\nI thought it would get better once I landed on my feet, and I did find some contract work, which is great, right? I'm really grateful for it. But... the worrying just never really let up. It's like my brain got stuck in that panicked mode from thirteen months ago and just forgot how to turn off. I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is, I really am trying to handle it. Um, concentrate? I mean, I think my focus is usually pretty good. I've always prided myself on being detail-oriented, especially in my field. You kind of have to be, you know? I don't want you to think I'm sitting at my desk just staring blankly at the screen or letting things slip through the cracks. I really try my best to stay on top of everything.

But... I guess, I don't know, I haven't really noticed a major problem with concentration. Like, I mentioned before that I've delayed some things because I get caught up worrying about what could go wrong, but that's more about wanting to do a good job than not being able to focus, right? I hope that's the right way to look at it. I just... I don't feel like my brain is foggy or anything. Does that sound okay? I really want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.

I'll be completely convinced I'm going to lose the contract, and then I start panicking about falling behind on my divorce payments, and then I'm spiraling into this thought that I'll never get back to a real leadership role again. \n\nI know it sounds so irrational when I say it out loud. I really hope you don't think I'm just being weak or over-dramatic. I just... I try to stop it, I really do, but it just takes over. Does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: Has this anxiety or worry started to interfere with your ability to focus at work, enjoy your free time, or manage your daily responsibilities?\n\nPatient: Um, interfere? I mean, I really try my best to keep everything running smoothly. I don't want to be someone who just drops the ball, you know? I still show up and put the hours in. \n\nBut... if I have to be completely honest, and I know I'm supposed to be... it has been affecting things a bit. I hope you don't judge me for this, because I usually pride myself on my work, but I've actually missed a few networking follow-ups lately. And I've even delayed some client deliverables. It's not that I'm slacking off, I promise! It's just... I get so stuck anticipating every single thing that could possibly go wrong, and I just kind of freeze up trying to prevent it all. \n\nAnd with my free time... well, I've sort of pulled back from playing soccer and hanging out with my friends. I'm just really embarrassed by how tense and irritable I've become. I don't want to snap at anyone or bring the mood down, so it's just easier to stay away. I hope that makes sense? I just want to be sure I'm giving you the right information, I really don't want to sound like a complete mess who can't do his job. Is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced sudden episodes where you feel intense fear or discomfort that peaks within minutes, maybe with symptoms like a pounding heart, feeling short of breath, or like you're losing control?\n\nPatient: Um, sudden episodes of intense fear? Like a panic attack or something? No, no, I don't think I've ever had anything like that. I mean, my heart might beat a little fast if I'm really stressed about a deadline, but that's just normal stress, right? I definitely haven't had anything where I felt like I couldn't breathe or was losing control. \n\nI really try to keep myself together, you know? Even when my brain is racing, I don't just completely lose it. I hope that's a good thing? I just want to make sure I'm answering you right. But no, I haven't had any of those sudden, intense episodes you're describing. I'm just dealing with the constant worrying, mostly. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, with the constant worrying and difficulty controlling your anxiety?\n\nPatient: Um, how long? Well, I mean, I've always been a bit of a planner, you know? Someone who likes to be prepared. I think that's usually a good trait for a manager. But... this level of worry, where it feels like it's running me instead of me running it? \n\nI guess... if I'm looking back, it really started getting bad about thirteen months ago. That's... well, I hope you don't judge me for this, but that's when I was let go from my old job. I mean, it was a restructuring thing, not because I was terrible at what I did, I promise! But yeah, getting fired really threw me. \n\nI thought it would get better once I landed on my feet, and I did find some contract work, which is great, right? I'm really grateful for it. But... the worrying just never really let up. It's like my brain got stuck in that panicked mode from thirteen months ago and just forgot how to turn off. I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is, I really am trying to handle it. Does that timeline make sense for what you're looking for?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or like you’re constantly on edge, even when there’s no immediate reason to be worried?\n\nPatient: Um, restless? I mean... I guess I do have a lot of energy, which can be a good thing, right? It keeps me moving. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around doing nothing. \n\nBut... yeah, if I'm being completely honest, I do feel pretty wound up most of the day. It's like my shoulders are just permanently locked up, almost like I'm bracing myself for some kind of bad news, even when everything is perfectly fine. I hope that doesn't sound too crazy. \n\nAnd I've noticed... well, I tend to pace around the house a lot. Or, if I'm on a work call, my leg is just constantly bouncing. Even when there's absolutely no reason to be stressed, I just can't seem to sit still. It's not that I'm trying to be disruptive or anything, I promise! It's just this nervous energy I can't shake. Is that... is that the kind of restlessness you mean? I hope I'm explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble sleeping because your mind is too active or you're feeling anxious, even when there's no immediate problem?\n\nPatient: Um, sleep... well, I mean, I try to get a full eight hours, I really do. I know how important sleep is for staying sharp, especially with contract work. I don't want you to think I'm just staying up all night being irresponsible or anything. \n\nBut... yeah, it can be a little tricky sometimes. I hope it's normal to have a little trouble winding down? Most nights, I just kind of lie there, and my brain starts replaying, like, answers I gave in interviews, or I'll just find myself staring at the clock at two or three in the morning. It's not that I'm trying to worry, it just sort of happens. \n\nAnd then, even when I do manage to get what should be a good amount of sleep... I wake up feeling exhausted. It's almost like I spent the whole night on call for a major system outage, you know? Just totally drained. I really hope I'm not making it sound like I'm complaining too much. Is that the kind of sleep trouble you're asking about? I want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Do you often experience muscle tension or physical discomfort, like headaches or stomachaches, that seem to be related to your anxiety?\n\nPatient: Um, muscle tension? I mean, I guess everyone gets a little stiff now and then, right? Especially when you work on computers all day. I try to make sure my posture is good, I really do. I don't want you to think I'm just slouching over a keyboard for twelve hours straight. \n\nBut... I don't know, I haven't really noticed anything like bad stomachaches or, you know, terrible headaches. Maybe a little tightness in my neck sometimes? But that's just normal stress from work, I think. I hope that's an okay answer? I just... I haven't really paid attention to anything major like that. Does that mean I'm doing okay in that area?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your anxiety has been affecting your ability to interact with others, like avoiding social situations or feeling like you can’t connect with people the way you used to?\n\nPatient: Um, social situations? I mean, I still try to be friendly and professional, I really do. I don't want you to think I'm just hiding in my house or being rude to people. I always try to keep up a good front.\n\nBut... yeah, I guess things have been a little harder lately. I hope it's okay to admit that? I've actually missed a few networking follow-ups recently... and, um, I've even delayed some client deliverables. It's not because I'm lazy or don't care about my work, I promise! It's just... I get so stuck anticipating every single little thing that could possibly go wrong, and I just freeze up. I really hope you don't think less of me for that.\n\nAnd outside of work... well, I used to play soccer and hang out with friends a lot more. But lately, I've been pulling back from all of that. I just... I get so embarrassed by how tense and irritable I've been feeling, and I don't want to bring everyone else down, you know? I want to be the guy everyone can rely on, not the guy who's always stressed out. Is it normal to pull away like that when you're feeling this wound up? I hope I'm not doing too much damage to my relationships.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel unusually tired or low on energy, even when you haven't been physically active or have gotten enough rest?\n\nPatient: Um, tired? Well, I mean, I guess everyone gets a little tired as they get older, right? I'm 47, so I don't expect to have the energy of a twenty-year-old anymore. I really try to stay active when I can, I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around doing nothing all day. \n\nBut... I don't know, I haven't really noticed feeling unusually tired, at least not in a way that seems abnormal. Like I mentioned before, I wake up feeling pretty drained sometimes, but I just chalk that up to the sleep issues, not some kind of mysterious lack of energy. I hope that makes sense? I'm trying to be as accurate as I can for you. Does that sound like a normal amount of tiredness to you?\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to concentrate or stay focused on tasks, especially when you’re feeling anxious or worried?\n\nPatient: Um, concentrate? I mean, I think my focus is usually pretty good. I've always prided myself on being detail-oriented, especially in my field. You kind of have to be, you know? I don't want you to think I'm sitting at my desk just staring blankly at the screen or letting things slip through the cracks. I really try my best to stay on top of everything.\n\nBut... I guess, I don't know, I haven't really noticed a major problem with concentration. Like, I mentioned before that I've delayed some things because I get caught up worrying about what could go wrong, but that's more about wanting to do a good job than not being able to focus, right? I hope that's the right way to look at it. I just... I don't feel like my brain is foggy or anything. Does that sound okay? I really want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your anxiety and worry are present most days, even when there isn’t anything specific happening to cause them?\n\nPatient: Um, most days? I mean, I really try to keep things in perspective. I know I have a lot to be thankful for, and I don't want to sound like I'm just complaining about every little thing. I try to stay positive, I really do. \n\nBut... yeah, I guess if I'm being completely honest, it does feel like it's there almost every day. I hope it's okay to admit that. It's just... my mind kind of runs away from me. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, significant distress? Well, I mean, I try really, really hard not to let it affect my day-to-day life. I pride myself on being reliable, especially professionally. I don't want you to think I'm just dropping the ball everywhere or giving up. 

But... I guess if I look at it honestly, it has made things a bit difficult lately. I'm a little ashamed to admit this, but I've missed a few networking follow-ups... and I have delayed some client deliverables. It's definitely not because I don't care! It's just... I get so stuck anticipating everything that could possibly go wrong, and I want to make sure I've thought of every angle before I send anything out. I hope you can understand that? I just want to do a good job.

And, um, as for my personal life... I guess I have pulled back quite a bit. I haven't been going to my soccer games, and I've been avoiding seeing friends. I'm just... I'm a little embarrassed, honestly, by how tense and irritable I've become. I don't want to be a burden or snap at anyone, so it's just easier to stay away so they don't see me like that. I really hope that doesn't make me sound like a terrible friend or a bad worker. I'm trying my absolute best to manage it all... does that make sense? Is that the kind of thing you were asking about?

It'll start with just one little concern, you know? Like, a client not responding to an email. And I know I should just wait it out, but within minutes, my brain just runs away with it. I'll be completely convinced I'm going to lose the contract, and then I start panicking about falling behind on my divorce payments, and then I'm spiraling into this thought that I'll never get back to a real leadership role again. \n\nI know it sounds so irrational when I say it out loud. I really hope you don't think I'm just being weak or over-dramatic. I just... I try to stop it, I really do, but it just takes over. Does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: Has this anxiety or worry started to interfere with your ability to focus at work, enjoy your free time, or manage your daily responsibilities?\n\nPatient: Um, interfere? I mean, I really try my best to keep everything running smoothly. I don't want to be someone who just drops the ball, you know? I still show up and put the hours in. \n\nBut... if I have to be completely honest, and I know I'm supposed to be... it has been affecting things a bit. I hope you don't judge me for this, because I usually pride myself on my work, but I've actually missed a few networking follow-ups lately. And I've even delayed some client deliverables. It's not that I'm slacking off, I promise! It's just... I get so stuck anticipating every single thing that could possibly go wrong, and I just kind of freeze up trying to prevent it all. \n\nAnd with my free time... well, I've sort of pulled back from playing soccer and hanging out with my friends. I'm just really embarrassed by how tense and irritable I've become. I don't want to snap at anyone or bring the mood down, so it's just easier to stay away. I hope that makes sense? I just want to be sure I'm giving you the right information, I really don't want to sound like a complete mess who can't do his job. Is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced sudden episodes where you feel intense fear or discomfort that peaks within minutes, maybe with symptoms like a pounding heart, feeling short of breath, or like you're losing control?\n\nPatient: Um, sudden episodes of intense fear? Like a panic attack or something? No, no, I don't think I've ever had anything like that. I mean, my heart might beat a little fast if I'm really stressed about a deadline, but that's just normal stress, right? I definitely haven't had anything where I felt like I couldn't breathe or was losing control. \n\nI really try to keep myself together, you know? Even when my brain is racing, I don't just completely lose it. I hope that's a good thing? I just want to make sure I'm answering you right. But no, I haven't had any of those sudden, intense episodes you're describing. I'm just dealing with the constant worrying, mostly. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, with the constant worrying and difficulty controlling your anxiety?\n\nPatient: Um, how long? Well, I mean, I've always been a bit of a planner, you know? Someone who likes to be prepared. I think that's usually a good trait for a manager. But... this level of worry, where it feels like it's running me instead of me running it? \n\nI guess... if I'm looking back, it really started getting bad about thirteen months ago. That's... well, I hope you don't judge me for this, but that's when I was let go from my old job. I mean, it was a restructuring thing, not because I was terrible at what I did, I promise! But yeah, getting fired really threw me. \n\nI thought it would get better once I landed on my feet, and I did find some contract work, which is great, right? I'm really grateful for it. But... the worrying just never really let up. It's like my brain got stuck in that panicked mode from thirteen months ago and just forgot how to turn off. I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is, I really am trying to handle it. Does that timeline make sense for what you're looking for?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or like you’re constantly on edge, even when there’s no immediate reason to be worried?\n\nPatient: Um, restless? I mean... I guess I do have a lot of energy, which can be a good thing, right? It keeps me moving. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around doing nothing. \n\nBut... yeah, if I'm being completely honest, I do feel pretty wound up most of the day. It's like my shoulders are just permanently locked up, almost like I'm bracing myself for some kind of bad news, even when everything is perfectly fine. I hope that doesn't sound too crazy. \n\nAnd I've noticed... well, I tend to pace around the house a lot. Or, if I'm on a work call, my leg is just constantly bouncing. Even when there's absolutely no reason to be stressed, I just can't seem to sit still. It's not that I'm trying to be disruptive or anything, I promise! It's just this nervous energy I can't shake. I hope I'm explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble sleeping because your mind is too active or you're feeling anxious, even when there's no immediate problem?\n\nPatient: Um, sleep... well, I mean, I try to get a full eight hours, I really do. Um, persistent when the events are gone? I... I'm not really sure I've noticed that. I mean, I try really hard to be logical about things. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around inventing things to panic about when everything is perfectly fine. I hope I'm explaining this okay... is that the kind of thing you usually help people with?\n\nDoctor: When your mind starts racing like that, do you notice any physical symptoms—like a fast heartbeat, sweating, trembling, or shortness of breath?\n\nPatient: Um, physical symptoms... I mean, nothing too crazy, I don't think. Sometimes my chest feels a little tight, maybe? But I'm sure that's just normal stress, right? I try not to make a big deal out of it. I wouldn't say I'm sweating or trembling or anything dramatic like that. Just... maybe a little tense. Is that what you mean? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you worry a lot, even when there isn’t an immediate problem, and does it feel hard to control those worries once they start?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I suppose I do worry a fair bit, even when things are technically fine. I really hope I don't sound like I'm just looking for things to complain about, because I know I should be able to handle this. But, yes, it is really hard to control once it gets going. \n\nMy mind just keeps jumping from one thing to another. Like, I'll be looking at contract work, and then suddenly I'm stressing about mortgage payments, and then... well, it’s a little embarrassing to admit, but I start worrying about whether people in the Bethesda tech circles think I’m, you know, damaged goods. I know it's silly to care so much about what other people think, but I do. And if a recruiter delays an email, even just by a day, it can feel like absolute proof that my whole career is just closing down. \n\nI try so hard to just be logical about it and snap out of it, but I can't seem to reel it back in once it starts. Am I making sense? I just hope I'm not blowing things completely out of proportion.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you feel anxious or worried most days, even when there isn’t anything specific happening to cause it?\n\nPatient: Um, most days? I mean, I guess I do feel a little on edge pretty often. But I really try not to let it get to me, you know? I don't want to be one of those people who just complains about everything when there are people out there with real problems. \n\nI suppose, if I'm being completely honest... yeah, it's there a lot of the time. Even on a quiet Sunday when I should just be relaxing, I'll catch myself feeling this sort of low-level dread. But I'm sure everyone feels that way sometimes, right? Especially with everything going on in the world. I just... I don't want to make it sound worse than it is. I hope that answers your question okay? I just want to make sure I'm giving you an accurate picture without, you know, exaggerating.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying about a variety of different things—like work, relationships, health, or everyday responsibilities—even when one specific problem gets resolved?\n\nPatient: Um, a variety of things? I guess, maybe a little bit. I mean, I don't think I worry about everything under the sun, you know? I try to keep things in perspective. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around obsessing over every little detail of my life. \n\nBut... well, I suppose even if I get a contract sorted out, I don't really feel relieved for very long. It's like my brain just looks for the next thing to focus on. Like, okay, work is fine, but what about the house? Or, you know, my health, or if I'm keeping up with friends enough. It just kind of shifts gears. I'm sure that's just a normal part of getting older and having responsibilities, right? Everyone has a lot on their plate. I just... I hope I'm not making it sound like I can't handle my life. Is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Can you describe how much control you feel you have over your anxiety and worries when they come up?\n\nPatient: Um, control? I mean, I try really hard to keep a lid on it, I promise I do. I don't want to sound like I'm completely falling apart or anything, because I know I need to just push through. But... if I'm being totally honest, that's actually kind of why I came in. \n\nI just... I can't seem to shut my brain off anymore. It's like I have zero brakes once it gets going. It'll start with just one little concern, you know? Like, a client not responding to an email. And I know I should just wait it out, but within minutes, my brain just runs away with it. I'll be completely convinced I'm going to lose the contract, and then I start panicking about falling behind on my divorce payments, and then I'm spiraling into this thought that I'll never get back to a real leadership role again. \n\nI know it sounds so irrational when I say it out loud. I really hope you don't think I'm just being weak or over-dramatic. I just... I try to stop it, I really do, but it just takes over. Does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: Has this anxiety or worry started to interfere with your ability to focus at work, enjoy your free time, or manage your daily responsibilities?\n\nPatient: Um, interfere? I mean, I really try my best to keep everything running smoothly. I don't want to be someone who just drops the ball, you know? I still show up and put the hours in. \n\nBut... if I have to be completely honest, and I know I'm supposed to be... it has been affecting things a bit. I hope you don't judge me for this, because I usually pride myself on my work, but I've actually missed a few networking follow-ups lately. And I've even delayed some client deliverables. It's not that I'm slacking off, I promise! It's just... I get so stuck anticipating every single thing that could possibly go wrong, and I just kind of freeze up trying to prevent it all. \n\nAnd with my free time... well, I've sort of pulled back from playing soccer and hanging out with my friends. I'm just really embarrassed by how tense and irritable I've become. I don't want to snap at anyone or bring the mood down, so it's just easier to stay away. I hope that makes sense? I just want to be sure I'm giving you the right information, I really don't want to sound like a complete mess who can't do his job. Is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced sudden episodes where you feel intense fear or discomfort that peaks within minutes, maybe with symptoms like a pounding heart, feeling short of breath, or like you're losing control?\n\nPatient: Um, sudden episodes of intense fear? Like a panic attack or something? No, no, I don't think I've ever had anything like that. I mean, my heart might beat a little fast if I'm really stressed about a deadline, but that's just normal stress, right? I definitely haven't had anything where I felt like I couldn't breathe or was losing control. \n\nI really try to keep myself together, you know? Even when my brain is racing, I don't just completely lose it. I hope that's a good thing? I just want to make sure I'm answering you right. But no, I haven't had any of those sudden, intense episodes you're describing. I'm just dealing with the constant worrying, mostly. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, with the constant worrying and difficulty controlling your anxiety?\n\nPatient: Um, how long? Well, I mean, I've always been a bit of a planner, you know? Someone who likes to be prepared. I think that's usually a good trait for a manager. But... this level of worry, where it feels like it's running me instead of me running it? \n\nI guess... if I'm looking back, it really started getting bad about thirteen months ago. That's... well, I hope you don't judge me for this, but that's when I was let go from my old job. I mean, it was a restructuring thing, not because I was terrible at what I did, I promise! But yeah, getting fired really threw me. \n\nI thought it would get better once I landed on my feet, and I did find some contract work, which is great, right? I'm really grateful for it. But... the worrying just never really let up. It's like my brain got stuck in that panicked mode from thirteen months ago and just forgot how to turn off. I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is, I really am trying to handle it. Does that timeline make sense for what you're looking for?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or like you’re constantly on edge, even when there’s no immediate reason to be worried?\n\nPatient: Um, restless? I mean... I guess I do have a lot of energy, which can be a good thing, right? It keeps me moving. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around doing nothing. \n\nBut... yeah, if I'm being completely honest, I do feel pretty wound up most of the day. It's like my shoulders are just permanently locked up, almost like I'm bracing myself for some kind of bad news, even when everything is perfectly fine. I hope that doesn't sound too crazy. \n\nAnd I've noticed... well, I tend to pace around the house a lot. Or, if I'm on a work call, my leg is just constantly bouncing. Even when there's absolutely no reason to be stressed, I just can't seem to sit still. It's not that I'm trying to be disruptive or anything, I promise! It's just this nervous energy I can't shake. Is that... is that the kind of restlessness you mean? I hope I'm explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble sleeping because your mind is too active or you're feeling anxious, even when there's no immediate problem?\n\nPatient: Um, sleep... well, I mean, I try to get a full eight hours, I really do. I know how important sleep is for staying sharp, especially with contract work. I don't want you to think I'm just staying up all night being irresponsible or anything. \n\nBut... yeah, it can be a little tricky sometimes. I hope it's normal to have a little trouble winding down? Most nights, I just kind of lie there, and my brain starts replaying, like, answers I gave in interviews, or I'll just find myself staring at the clock at two or three in the morning. It's not that I'm trying to worry, it just sort of happens. \n\nAnd then, even when I do manage to get what should be a good amount of sleep... I wake up feeling exhausted. It's almost like I spent the whole night on call for a major system outage, you know? Just totally drained. I really hope I'm not making it sound like I'm complaining too much. Is that the kind of sleep trouble you're asking about? I want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Do you often experience muscle tension or physical discomfort, like headaches or stomachaches, that seem to be related to your anxiety?\n\nPatient: Um, muscle tension? I don't know, I haven't really noticed anything like bad stomachaches or, you know, terrible headaches. Maybe a little tightness in my neck sometimes? Does that mean I'm doing okay in that area?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your anxiety has been affecting your ability to interact with others, like avoiding social situations or feeling like you can’t connect with people the way you used to?\n\nPatient: Um, social situations? I always try to keep up a good front.\n\nBut... yeah, I guess things have been a little harder lately. I hope it's okay to admit that? I get so stuck anticipating every single little thing that could possibly go wrong, and I just freeze up. I really hope you don't think less of me for that.\n\nAnd outside of work... well, I used to play soccer and hang out with friends a lot more. But lately, I've been pulling back from all of that. I just... I get so embarrassed by how tense and irritable I've been feeling, and I don't want to bring everyone else down, you know? I want to be the guy everyone can rely on, not the guy who's always stressed out. Is it normal to pull away like that when you're feeling this wound up? I hope I'm not doing too much damage to my relationships.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel unusually tired or low on energy, even when you haven't been physically active or have gotten enough rest?\n\nPatient: Um, tired? Well, I mean, I guess everyone gets a little tired as they get older, right? I'm 47, so I don't expect to have the energy of a twenty-year-old anymore. I really try to stay active when I can, I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around doing nothing all day. \n\nBut... I don't know, I haven't really noticed feeling unusually tired, at least not in a way that seems abnormal. I'm trying to be as accurate as I can for you. Does that sound like a normal amount of tiredness to you?\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to concentrate or stay focused on tasks, especially when you’re feeling anxious or worried?\n\nPatient: Um, concentrate? Does that mean I'm doing okay in that area?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your anxiety has been affecting your ability to interact with others, like avoiding social situations or feeling like you can’t connect with people the way you used to?\n\nPatient: Um, social situations? I always try to keep up a good front.\n\nBut... yeah, I guess things have been a little harder lately. I hope it's okay to admit that? I get so stuck anticipating every single little thing that could possibly go wrong, and I just freeze up. I really hope you don't think less of me for that.\n\nAnd outside of work... well, I used to play soccer and hang out with friends a lot more. But lately, I've been pulling back from all of that. I just... I get so embarrassed by how tense and irritable I've been feeling, and I don't want to bring everyone else down, you know? I want to be the guy everyone can rely on, not the guy who's always stressed out. Is it normal to pull away like that when you're feeling this wound up? I hope I'm not doing too much damage to my relationships.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel unusually tired or low on energy, even when you haven't been physically active or have gotten enough rest?\n\nPatient: Um, tired? I really try to stay active when I can, I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around doing nothing all day. \n\nBut... I don't know, I haven't really noticed feeling unusually tired, at least not in a way that seems abnormal. I'm trying to be as accurate as I can for you. I mean, I think my focus is usually pretty good. I've always prided myself on being detail-oriented, especially in my field. You kind of have to be, you know? I don't want you to think I'm sitting at my desk just staring blankly at the screen or letting things slip through the cracks. I really try my best to stay on top of everything.\n\nBut... I guess, I don't know, I haven't really noticed a major problem with concentration. Like, I mentioned before that I've delayed some things because I get caught up worrying about what could go wrong, but that's more about wanting to do a good job than not being able to focus, right? I hope that's the right way to look at it. I just... I don't feel like my brain is foggy or anything. Does that sound okay? I really want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your anxiety and worry are present most days, even when there isn’t anything specific happening to cause them?\n\nPatient: Um, most days? Um, sudden episodes of intense fear out of nowhere? No, I don't think so. I mean, I really try to keep a level head, and I don't want you to think I'm prone to just... losing control or panicking for absolutely no reason. I like to think I'm usually pretty grounded, even when I'm stressed.

It's not that I'm slacking off, I promise! It's just... I get so stuck anticipating every single thing that could possibly go wrong, and I just kind of freeze up trying to prevent it all. \n\nAnd with my free time... well, I've sort of pulled back from playing soccer and hanging out with my friends. I'm just really embarrassed by how tense and irritable I've become. I don't want to snap at anyone or bring the mood down, so it's just easier to stay away. I hope that makes sense? I just want to be sure I'm giving you the right information, I really don't want to sound like a complete mess who can't do his job. Is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced sudden episodes where you feel intense fear or discomfort that peaks within minutes, maybe with symptoms like a pounding heart, feeling short of breath, or like you're losing control?\n\nPatient: Um, sudden episodes of intense fear? Like a panic attack or something? No, no, I don't think I've ever had anything like that. I mean, my heart might beat a little fast if I'm really stressed about a deadline, but that's just normal stress, right? I definitely haven't had anything where I felt like I couldn't breathe or was losing control. \n\nI really try to keep myself together, you know? Even when my brain is racing, I don't just completely lose it. I hope that's a good thing? I just want to make sure I'm answering you right. But no, I haven't had any of those sudden, intense episodes you're describing. I'm just dealing with the constant worrying, mostly. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, with the constant worrying and difficulty controlling your anxiety?\n\nPatient: Um, how long? Well, I mean, I've always been a bit of a planner, you know? Someone who likes to be prepared. I think that's usually a good trait for a manager. But... this level of worry, where it feels like it's running me instead of me running it? \n\nI guess... if I'm looking back, it really started getting bad about thirteen months ago. That's... well, I hope you don't judge me for this, but that's when I was let go from my old job. I mean, it was a restructuring thing, not because I was terrible at what I did, I promise! But yeah, getting fired really threw me. \n\nI thought it would get better once I landed on my feet, and I did find some contract work, which is great, right? I'm really grateful for it. But... the worrying just never really let up. It's like my brain got stuck in that panicked mode from thirteen months ago and just forgot how to turn off. I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is, I really am trying to handle it. Does that timeline make sense for what you're looking for?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or like you’re constantly on edge, even when there’s no immediate reason to be worried?\n\nPatient: Um, restless? I mean... I guess I do have a lot of energy, which can be a good thing, right? It keeps me moving. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around doing nothing. \n\nBut... yeah, if I'm being completely honest, I do feel pretty wound up most of the day. It's like my shoulders are just permanently locked up, almost like I'm bracing myself for some kind of bad news, even when everything is perfectly fine. I hope that doesn't sound too crazy. \n\nAnd I've noticed... well, I tend to pace around the house a lot. Or, if I'm on a work call, my leg is just constantly bouncing. Even when there's absolutely no reason to be stressed, I just can't seem to sit still. It's not that I'm trying to be disruptive or anything, I promise! It's just this nervous energy I can't shake. Is that... is that the kind of restlessness you mean? I hope I'm explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble sleeping because your mind is too active or you're feeling anxious, even when there's no immediate problem?\n\nPatient: Um, sleep... well, I mean, I try to get a full eight hours, I really do. I know how important sleep is for staying sharp, especially with contract work. I don't want you to think I'm just staying up all night being irresponsible or anything. \n\nBut... yeah, it can be a little tricky sometimes. I hope it's normal to have a little trouble winding down? Most nights, I just kind of lie there, and my brain starts replaying, like, answers I gave in interviews, or I'll just find myself staring at the clock at two or three in the morning. It's not that I'm trying to worry, it just sort of happens. \n\nAnd then, even when I do manage to get what should be a good amount of sleep... I wake up feeling exhausted. It's almost like I spent the whole night on call for a major system outage, you know? Just totally drained. I really hope I'm not making it sound like I'm complaining too much. Is that the kind of sleep trouble you're asking about? I want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Do you often experience muscle tension or physical discomfort, like headaches or stomachaches, that seem to be related to your anxiety?\n\nPatient: Um, muscle tension? I mean, I guess everyone gets a little stiff now and then, right? Especially when you work on computers all day. I try to make sure my posture is good, I really do. I don't want you to think I'm just slouching over a keyboard for twelve hours straight. \n\nBut... I don't know, I haven't really noticed anything like bad stomachaches or, you know, terrible headaches. Maybe a little tightness in my neck sometimes? But that's just normal stress from work, I think. I hope that's an okay answer? I just... I haven't really paid attention to anything major like that. Does that mean I'm doing okay in that area?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your anxiety has been affecting your ability to interact with others, like avoiding social situations or feeling like you can’t connect with people the way you used to?\n\nPatient: Um, social situations? I mean, I still try to be friendly and professional, I really do. I don't want you to think I'm just hiding in my house or being rude to people. I always try to keep up a good front.\n\nBut... yeah, I guess things have been a little harder lately. I hope it's okay to admit that? I've actually missed a few networking follow-ups recently... and, um, I've even delayed some client deliverables. It's not because I'm lazy or don't care about my work, I promise! It's just... I get so stuck anticipating every single little thing that could possibly go wrong, and I just freeze up. I really hope you don't think less of me for that.\n\nAnd outside of work... well, I used to play soccer and hang out with friends a lot more. But lately, I've been pulling back from all of that. I just... I get so embarrassed by how tense and irritable I've been feeling, and I don't want to bring everyone else down, you know? I want to be the guy everyone can rely on, not the guy who's always stressed out. Is it normal to pull away like that when you're feeling this wound up? I hope I'm not doing too much damage to my relationships.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel unusually tired or low on energy, even when you haven't been physically active or have gotten enough rest?\n\nPatient: Um, tired? Well, I mean, I guess everyone gets a little tired as they get older, right? I'm 47, so I don't expect to have the energy of a twenty-year-old anymore. I really try to stay active when I can, I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around doing nothing all day. \n\nBut... I don't know, I haven't really noticed feeling unusually tired, at least not in a way that seems abnormal. Like I mentioned before, I wake up feeling pretty drained sometimes, but I just chalk that up to the sleep issues, not some kind of mysterious lack of energy. I hope that makes sense? I'm trying to be as accurate as I can for you. Does that sound like a normal amount of tiredness to you?\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to concentrate or stay focused on tasks, especially when you’re feeling anxious or worried?\n\nPatient: Um, concentrate? I mean, I think my focus is usually pretty good. I've always prided myself on being detail-oriented, especially in my field. You kind of have to be, you know? I don't want you to think I'm sitting at my desk just staring blankly at the screen or letting things slip through the cracks. I really try my best to stay on top of everything.\n\nBut... I guess, I don't know, I haven't really noticed a major problem with concentration. Like, I mentioned before that I've delayed some things because I get caught up worrying about what could go wrong, but that's more about wanting to do a good job than not being able to focus, right? I hope that's the right way to look at it. I just... I don't feel like my brain is foggy or anything. Does that sound okay? I really want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your anxiety and worry are present most days, even when there isn’t anything specific happening to cause them?\n\nPatient: Um, most days? I mean, I really try to keep things in perspective. I know I have a lot to be thankful for, and I don't want to sound like I'm just complaining about every little thing. I try to stay positive, I really do. \n\nBut... yeah, I guess if I'm being completely honest, it does feel like it's there almost every day. I hope it's okay to admit that. It's just... my mind kind of runs away from me. Like, it keeps jumping from my contract work to the mortgage payments, and then I start worrying about whether people in the Bethesda tech circles think I’m, you know, damaged goods or something. I know I shouldn't let my thoughts spiral like that. I really hope you don't think I'm just being dramatic. Is it normal to have your mind jump around to all these different things like that?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your anxiety has caused you significant distress or has made it hard for you to function in your daily life, like at work or in your personal relationships?\n\nPatient: Um, significant distress? Well, I mean, I try really, really hard not to let it affect my day-to-day life. I pride myself on being reliable, especially professionally. I don't want you to think I'm just dropping the ball everywhere or giving up. \n\nBut... I guess if I look at it honestly, it has made things a bit difficult lately. I'm a little ashamed to admit this, but I've missed a few networking follow-ups... and I have delayed some client deliverables. It's definitely not because I don't care! It's just... I get so stuck anticipating everything that could possibly go wrong, and I want to make sure I've thought of every angle before I send anything out. I hope you can understand that? I really hope that doesn't make me sound like a terrible friend or a bad worker. I'm trying my absolute best to manage it all... does that make sense? Is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you feel anxious or worried about a number of different things, not just specific events or situations, and that this anxiety is persistent even when those specific events are no longer present?\n\nPatient: Um, persistent when the events are gone? I really want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your anxiety and worry are present most days, even when there isn’t anything specific happening to cause them?\n\nPatient: Um, most days? I mean, I really try to keep things in perspective. I know I have a lot to be thankful for, and I don't want to sound like I'm just complaining about every little thing. I try to stay positive, I really do. \n\nBut... yeah, I guess if I'm being completely honest, it does feel like it's there almost every day. I hope it's okay to admit that. It's just... my mind kind of runs away from me. Like, it keeps jumping from my contract work to the mortgage payments, and then I start worrying about whether people in the Bethesda tech circles think I’m, you know, damaged goods or something. And if a recruiter delays an email, even just by a day, it can suddenly feel like proof that my whole career is closing down. It sounds a bit silly when I say it out loud, doesn't it? I know I shouldn't let my thoughts spiral like that. I really hope you don't think I'm just being dramatic. Is it normal to have your mind jump around to all these different things like that?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your anxiety has caused you significant distress or has made it hard for you to function in your daily life, like at work or in your personal relationships?\n\nPatient: Um, significant distress? Well, I mean, I try really, really hard not to let it affect my day-to-day life. I pride myself on being reliable, especially professionally. I don't want you to think I'm just dropping the ball everywhere or giving up. \n\nBut... I guess if I look at it honestly, it has made things a bit difficult lately. I'm a little ashamed to admit this, but I've missed a few networking follow-ups... and I have delayed some client deliverables. It's definitely not because I don't care! It's just... I get so stuck anticipating everything that could possibly go wrong, and I want to make sure I've thought of every angle before I send anything out. I hope you can understand that? I just want to do a good job.\n\nAnd, um, as for my personal life... I guess I have pulled back quite a bit. I haven't been going to my soccer games, and I've been avoiding seeing friends. I'm just... I'm a little embarrassed, honestly, by how tense and irritable I've become. I don't want to be a burden or snap at anyone, so it's just easier to stay away so they don't see me like that. I really hope that doesn't make me sound like a terrible friend or a bad worker. I'm trying my absolute best to manage it all... does that make sense? Is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you feel anxious or worried about a number of different things, not just specific events or situations, and that this anxiety is persistent even when those specific events are no longer present?\n\nPatient: Um, persistent when the events are gone? I... I'm not really sure I've noticed that. I mean, I try really hard to be logical about things. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around inventing things to panic about when everything is perfectly fine. I really try to appreciate the good moments and let things go once a problem is actually solved. \n\nLike, if a project finally wraps up or a software issue gets fixed, I think I feel relieved. I don't keep worrying about that specific problem once it's in the rearview mirror. It's just... I guess in my line of work, and with life in general, there's usually just a new, real problem waiting right behind the last one, you know? So I haven't really noticed the worry staying when there's absolutely nothing going on, because there's almost always *something* on the schedule to deal with. \n\nI hope that makes sense? I just want to make sure I'm giving you an accurate picture and not making myself sound completely irrational. Did I answer that okay for you?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced sudden, unexpected episodes where you felt intense fear or discomfort, even when nothing specific was happening to cause it?\n\nPatient: Um, sudden episodes of intense fear out of nowhere? No, I don't think so. I mean, I really try to keep a level head, and I don't want you to think I'm prone to just... losing control or panicking for absolutely no reason. I like to think I'm usually pretty grounded, even when I'm stressed. \n\nWhen I get overwhelmed, it's usually because I'm thinking about a specific deadline or a problem with my contract work, like I mentioned before. It doesn't just hit me out of the blue like a lightning bolt when I'm, say, relaxing on the couch or something. I mean, my mind might be racing about work, but it's tied to something real, not just a random wave of terror. \n\nI hope that's a good thing? I really try to hold it together and not let things escalate into full-blown panic. Does that sound like a normal way to handle it to you?"
